Let's think mathematical formulation for off-peak linear autocorrelation. Let a probability vector of length n. We want min max_{k=1}^{n-1} ∑_{i} a_i a_{i+k}. This is nonconvex. We can transform using convolution theorem. Let A(ω) = FFT(a). c_k = (1/N) ∑_ω |A(ω)|^2 e^{iωk} (with appropriate N). Maximizing? We want inverse spectrum low. Equivalent minimize L∞ norm of inverse of PSD. By duality, max c_k = min? There might be convex dual in power spectrum P>=0? Since c_k = inverse FFT(P)_k. Let's implement `fista_max` with:
```
x = normalized
best = max_off(x)
eta = 0.4
t = 1.0
x_prev = x
for iter:
  ac = autocorr(x) (full or off only? full for c0 maybe)
  off = ac[1:n]
  m = off.max()
  update best
  if m < tiny break
  logw = (off - m)/scale  # scale adaptive: frac*m + tiny
  # subtract logw.max for stability; p = exp(logw)/sum
  # if p has too many small, no matter
  # compute grad = weighted autocorr gradient using p (exclude 0)
  y = x + (t-1)/(t+1) (x - x_prev)
  y = project? We need y on or near simplex for gradient. Project y to simplex or clip normalize. Use project_simplex(y) every iter.
  grad = weighted_gradient(y,p)
  # step
  # Maybe use backtracking on LSE or exact max from y.
  # We can try a few eta candidates: current eta, 0.8eta, 0.5eta, 0.25eta, 1.25eta.
  base = y - eta*grad
  candidates = [base, base with eta*.5, ...]
  choose min exact max_off after projection.
  if best_new < current_cost - 1e-15 boost eta *=1.02 else if increased eta*0.9
  x = chosen
  t = 1+sqrt(1+4t^2)/2
```

```
x = feas
best = cost(x)
eta = 0.2
theta = 0.9
x_prev=x
stall=0
for iter in limit:
  b = conv_square(x)
  m = b.max()
  if m < best - tol: best=m; best_x=x; stall=0 else stall++
  p = weights(b, temp)
  # Nesterov point
  if use_mom:
     t = 1+sqrt(1+4*t^2)/2
     y = x + (t-1)/t*(x - x_prev)
  else: y=x
  y = project_simplex(y)
  # gradient at y with same p
  g = grad(y,p)
  # step candidates
  gnorm = g.max()
  if gnorm < 1e-300: break
  eta = clip(eta, 1e-6, 2.5)
  best_trial = y; best_cost = m_y? Need cost_y = conv_square(y).max() maybe compute before grad? We can compute b_y = conv_square(y) and m_y; if m_y < m, maybe just set x=y? But y from extrapolation may be bad. Choose candidate among y and y - eta g.
  candidates eta [eta, 0.5eta, 0.25eta, 1.5eta]
  for e: z=proj(y - e*g); c=cost(conv_square(z)); choose min c
      if c < m_y - 1e-14 accept, else if c < m - 1e-14 accept? We want decrease from x. Use threshold.
  if accepted:
      x_prev=x; x=best_z; eta *=1.01; t update
  else:
      x_prev=x; x unchanged; eta*=0.7; reset t; stall large
  if stall > restart: perturb best_x, reset
  temp update: temp = max(temp_min, temp*decay) or cosine schedule.
```

def propose_candidate(budget_s=1800.0):

  T0 = time.monotonic()
  time_limit = min(budget_s - 60, 1740)

  a_best = get_parent()

  n = len(a_best)

Let's code details.

`next_pow2(m)` using `1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()`.

`conv_square(a)`:
```
a = np.asarray(a,float64)
n=len(a)
size=2*n-1
L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:size]
c = np.maximum(c,0) # roundoff
return c
```
Note for complex? A*A not abs squared; it's polynomial square, can have negative? For nonnegative a, polynomial coefficients nonnegative, but FFT A*A may have roundoff producing small negative in IFFT. Clip at 0 okay. It should preserve sum of c = sum a^2? Actually sum coefficients = (sum a)^2. Clipping small negligible. If A*A with floating, for nonnegative real, coefficients should nonnegative. Good.

`scaled evaluate`:
```
def safe_eval(a):
   try:
      arr = np.asarray(a,float)
      arr = np.maximum(arr,0)
      s=arr.sum()
      if s < 0.01: return inf
      # maybe normalize? evaluate scale invariant; but if sum huge clipped? Use arr/s to be safe.
      lst = [float(x) for x in arr/s]
      return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
```
But if hidden objective not scale invariant due clipping? It is clipped at 1000 and zero; for normalized sum1 values ≤1, no clipping. Scale invariant. Good.

`norm_sum1`: `a=np.maximum(np.asarray(a,float),0); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-15 return ones/n; return a/s`.

`simplex_project`: Need robust for large n. Standard. Implementation:
```
def simplex_project(v):
    n=len(v)
    if n==0: return v
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u*np.arange(1,n+1) > (cssv-1))[0]
    if len(rho)==0: return np.ones(n)/n
    rho=rho[-1]
    theta=(cssv[rho]-1)/(rho+1.0)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
For sum 1. Good.

`weights_b(b, mask, temp, bg=0.0)`:
```
vals = np.where(mask, b, -np.inf)
m = vals.max()
if m <= 0: p = np.where(mask, 1.0/np.sum(mask), 0)
else:
   scale = max(m*temp, 1e-18)
   # for large b-m may under/overflow; use shifted
   logw = (vals - m)/scale
   logw = np.clip(logw, -100, 0) # since vals<=m; if mask false -inf -> -100? use where
   w = np.exp(logw)
   w[~mask]=0
   s=w.sum()
   if s<1e-30: p = np.where(mask, 1/np.sum(mask),0)
   else: p=w/s
   if bg>0:
       uniform = np.where(mask, 1.0/np.sum(mask),0)
       p = (1-bg)*p + bg*uniform
return p
```
If b has NaN? no. If mask all false? return full uniform.

`grad_weighted(a,p)`:

```

Conv c[m]=∑_s a_rev[s] p_rev[m-s]=∑_t a_t p[2n-2 - (m - (n-1-t))? Let's set t=n-1-s => s=n-1-t. p_rev[m-s]=p[(2n-2)-(m-s)] = p[2n-2-m+n-1-t] = p[3n-3 - m - t]. Not want.
Let's stick to proven: h_i = conv(a, p_rev)[2n-2 - i], where p_rev[t]=p[2n-2-t]. Derivation: c[m]=∑_j a_j p_rev[m-j]=∑ a_j p[2n-2-m+j]. Set m=2n-2-i -> p[i+j]. Yes. So use `a` (not rev) and `p_rev`. Puma function details:
```
def puma_search(start, time_limit, mask, temp_start=0.35, temp_end=0.02, eta_start=0.35, bg=0.02, restart_every=4000, verbose=True):
  t0=time.monotonic()
  x=norm_sum1(start)
  b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max(b,mask)
  best_m=m; best_x=x.copy(); best_b=b.copy()
  eta=eta_start; t=1.0; x_prev=x.copy(); stall=0; fail_count=0; it=0
  temp=temp_start
  # schedules: total iterations unknown; use time-based: fraction = (time-t0)/time_limit; temp = temp_start + (temp_end-temp_start)*fraction? better decay.
  total_lim=max(1,time_limit)
  while time-t0 < time_limit:
     frac=(time-t0)/total_lim
     temp = max(temp_end, temp_start * (1-frac) + temp_end*frac) # linear
     # maybe bg reduce later: bg_cur = bg*(1-frac*0.5)
     # Nesterov
     if it>1:
        t_new=(1+np.sqrt(1+4*t*t))/2
        theta=(t-1)/t_new
        if theta>0.9: theta=0.9
        y=x + theta*(x - x_prev)
        t=t_new
     else: y=x.copy(); t=1.0
     y = simplex_project(y)
     # If projection changes y significantly? okay
     p=weights_b(b, mask, temp, bg_cur)
     g=grad_weighted(y,p)
     gmax=np.max(g)
     if not finite or gmax<1e-300: break
     # Scale eta? maybe `eta = eta / max(1, temp*? )`? keep.
     accepted=False
     # Try three step factors
     for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
        e=eta*factor
        z=np.maximum(y - e*g, 0?) # simple clip? But projection after
        z=simplex_project(y - e*g)
        b_z=conv_square(z)
        m_z=masked_max(b_z,mask)
        if m_z < m - 1e-14:
            accepted=True; tol candidate
            if m_z < best_m - 1e-14:
                best_m=m_z; best_x=z.copy(); best_b=b_z.copy(); stall=0
            x_prev=x; x=z; b=b_z; m=m_z; eta=min(1.5, eta*1.01); break
        # If fail, maybe reduce? loop
     if not accepted:
        eta=max(0.01, eta*0.75)
        x_prev=x; t=1.0; fail_count+=1
     else:
        fail_count=0
     it+=1
     if stall > restart_every:
        # restart from best with noise
        rng = np.random.default_rng(1234 + stall)
        noise = 0.02/(1+0.1*(stall//restart_every))
        x_r = best_x + rng.normal(0, noise*n? , n)
        x_r = np.maximum(x_r, 0)
        s=x_r.sum(); if s>0 x_r/=s
        if masked_max(conv_square(x_r),mask) < m: x=x_r; b=...; m=...
        else: x=best_x.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); m=best_m
        x_prev=x.copy(); t=1; eta=eta_start; stall=0
     if verbose and it % 2000 ==0: print...
  return best_x,best_m
```
But `b` from x used for weights; if accept x=z, b updated. Good.

Need set `eta_start` maybe 0.2. If too large, line search fail; covered.

Could use `simplex_project` on y - e*g. Because g nonnegative, y-e*g sum <1, projection scales up. Good. But if y-e*g has large negative values, project sets some zero; okay.

`spectral_candidate_from_phase(a_phase, n, mask, gamma=1.0)`:
```
size=2n-1; L=pow2(size)
# target b flat over size
bt=np.zeros(L); bt[:size]=1.0/size
T=np.fft.fft(bt,L)
# complex sqrt hermitian
A=np.sqrt(T)
A=0.5*(A + np.conj(A[(-np.arange(L))%L]))
# blend with phase of a_phase
if a_phase is not None:
  Ap=np.fft.fft(np.pad(a_phase, (0,L-len(a_phase))), L)
  # Use magnitude from sqrt(T) and phase from Ap? Or blend angles
  mag=np.abs(A)
  phase=np.angle(Ap)
  A2=mag*np.exp(1j*phase)
  A2=0.5*(A2 + np.conj(A2[(-np.arange(L))%L]))
  # maybe average A and A2
  A = A*0.5 + A2*0.5; enforce hermitian
else: ...
a=np.fft.ifft(A).real[:n]
a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s>1e-12: a/=s
# apply a few positive iterations? maybe just candidate.
return a if finite else None
```

Alternative amplitude iteration:
```
def spectral_amplitude_iteration(a_start, n, L_iters=200):
  a=norm_sum1(a_start)
  L=pow2(2n-1)
  bt=flat; T=fft(bt); mag_target=np.sqrt(np.abs(T))
  for it in range(L_iters):
    A=fft(pad(a),L)
    # blend magnitude: mag = mag_target ** 1.0 (or lerp log)
    newA = mag_target * np.exp(1j*np.angle(A))
    newA=hermitian
    a_new=ifft(newA).real[:n]
    a_new=clip; normalize
    if cost(a_new)<cost(a): a=a_new
  return a
```

Local search design efficient:
```
def local_pair_search(a0, time_limit, mask, verbose=False):
  x=norm_sum1(a0)
  b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max(b,mask)
  best=x,best_m=m
  rng=default
  peak_count=50
  while time-left >...:
    # active top K from masked b
    active = [idx for idx in np.where(mask)[0] sorted by b desc top 40]
    # risk
    risk=np.zeros(n)
    for k in active:
       w = b[k]  # or normalized
       s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n,k+1)
       idxs=np.arange(s,e)
       risk[s:e] += 0.5*w * x[k-idxs]
    sources = top 50 by risk
    dests = bottom 50 by risk (or top 50 lowest)
    improved=False
    for trial in range(200):
       s_idx = sources[rand]; d=dests[rand]
       if s==d or x[s]<=1e-12: continue
       # line search sample fractions
       base_b = b.copy()? no, use active arrays
       # precompute for active arrays: b_active, val_s, val_d, quad flags
       # To avoid per δ allocation, loop active compute c_max
       best_delta=None; best_sur=m
       for frac in (0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7):
          δ=frac*x[s]
          sur=0
          for each active k:
             val_s = x[k-s] if valid else 0
             val_d = x[k-d] if valid else 0
             c = b[k] + δ*(val_d - val_s) - δ*δ*(1 if s+d==k else 0)? Wait factor? We used quad -2? Let's rederive with b=a^T Q a, Q entries 1 for ordered pairs. Δ=-δ e_s + δ e_d. Δ^T Q Δ = -2δ^2 if s≠d and s+d=k (because Q_s,d=Q_d,s=1). Yes -2δ^2. Linear=2δ((Q a)_d - (Q a)_s). (Q a)_i = ∑ Q_{i,j}a_j. For row i, valid j=k-i one (or one diagonal). So (Q a)_i = a_{k-i} if valid else 0, except if i=k/2, still a_i (not doubled). So linear=2δ(a_{k-d}-a_{k-s}). Thus c = b_k + 2δ(val_d - val_s) - 2δ^2*I(s+d==k). Use that.
          if sur < best_sur: best=...
       if best_sur < m*(1-1e-8):
          δ=best_delta
          x_trial = x; x_trial[s]-=δ; x_trial[d]+=δ; normalize? Operation preserves sum (δ), but due float; no need normalize if exact, but ensure sum. Normalize anyway.
          b_trial=conv_square(x_trial); m_trial=masked_max
          if m_trial < best_m - 1e-13:
             x=x_trial; b=b_trial; best_m=m_trial; improved=True
             # optionally update active/risk after some improvements
    if not improved: break or perturb
  return best_x,best_m
```

Can we exploit analytical KKT to polish? At optimum for mask full, active set A, there exist λ (sum), μ≥0 for nonneg such that for active coefficients weights ν_k≥0 sum1, 2∑ν_k Q_k a + λ1 - μ_a =0, with μ_i=0 if a_i>0. Thus for positive entries, `2∑ν_k a_{k-i} + λ` constant. This suggests we can solve forweights via fixed-point: Given a, active peaks; find ν from softmax; update a to make gradient flat: a_i = ? From KKT, for positive entries, g_i = constant - λ. We can project to simplex a = max(0, c - w_i) where w_i = gradient? Actually if g_i = 2∑ν Q_k a_i? The KKT says g_i + λ - μ_i=0 => μ_i = g_i+λ. For μ_i≥0. For positive a_i, μ_i=0 -> g_i constant = -λ. Since g_i nonnegative, λ negative. Thus a should be zero where g_i > C. Projection of something? Projected gradient step with sufficiently large η approximates `a ∝ max(0, 1 - α*g?)`. Hard.

def lp_fw_search(a0, time_limit, mask, solver, initial_K=120, max_K=300, verbose=True):
  x=norm_sum1(a0); b=conv; m=maskedmax
  best...
  no_improve=0
  K=initial_K
  while time<... and no_improve<300:
    active_indices = sorted top K of masked b (by b descending)
    # maybe active where b >= 0.9*m
    A = np.zeros((len(active), n))
    for r,k in enumerate(active): fill row
    zvar=cp.Variable(n, nonneg=True); Mvar=cp.Variable(nonneg=True)
    constraints=[cp.sum(zvar)==1, A @ zvar <= Mvar]
    # add <= current? Mvar <= m*? not
    prob=cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(Mvar), constraints)
    prob.solve(solver=solver, verbose=False)
    if status not optimal: K//=2; if K<20 break
    z=np.maximum(zvar.value,0); s=z.sum(); if s<1e-12 break; z/=s
    # line search
    alphas=[1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.2]
    best_alpha=None; best_c=m
    for alpha in alphas:
       trial=(1-alpha)*x + alpha*z; trial=np.maximum(trial,0); normalize? The combo sum 1 if normalized; clip may alter. normalize.
       c=maskedmax(conv_square(trial))
       if c<best_c: best_c=c; best_alpha=alpha
    if best_alpha is not None and best_c<m-1e-13:
       x=trial; m=best_c; update best; no_improve=0; K=min(max_K,K+5)
    else:
       no_improve+=1; K=max(initial_K//2,K//2); if no_improve>50: break? 
    # print every 20
  return best
```

`hermitian_enforce(A)`:
```
def enforce_hermitian(A):
    idx = (-np.arange(len(A))) % len(A)
    return 0.5*(A + np.conj(A[idx]))
```

def interpolate_sequence(f, new_n):
   f=norm_sum1(f)
   if new_n == len: return f
   old_x = np.linspace(0,1,len(f))
   new_x = np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
   g=np.interp(new_x, old_x, f)
   g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum(); return g/s
```

```
def puma_search(start, time_limit, mask, temp_start=0.35, temp_end=0.02, eta_start=0.30, bg=0.03, restart_every=6000, verbose=True):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    x=norm_sum1(start)
    x_prev=x.copy()
    b=conv_square(x)
    m=masked_max(b,mask)
    best_m=m; best_x=x.copy()
    eta=eta_start; t=1.0; it=0; stall=0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(2024)
    if time_limit<=0: return x,best_m
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        frac=(time.monotonic()-t0)/max(time_limit,1e-9)
        frac=min(1.0,frac)
        temp = temp_start + (temp_end-temp_start)*frac
        bg_cur = bg*(1-0.5*frac)  # reduce background later
        if it>1:
            t_new=(1+np.sqrt(1+4*t*t))/2
            theta=(t-1)/t_new
            if theta>0.9: theta=0.9
            y=x + theta*(x-x_prev)
            t=t_new
        else:
            y=x.copy(); t=1.0
        y=simplex_project(y)
        p=weights_b(b, mask, temp, bg_cur)
        g=grad_weighted(y,p)
        gmax=np.max(g)
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax < 1e-300:
            break
        # Adaptive eta based on gmax? We can set effective = eta / max(1.0, gmax*1000?)? Let's not.
        accepted=False
        # Ensure step not too huge: max_step = 0.05? raw shift.
        # We can cap e*g infinity norm to 0.05: e_cap = min(e, 0.05/gmax)
        for factor in (1.0,0.5,0.25):
            e=eta*factor
            # cap individual change at 0.03 maybe
            cap=0.03
            if gmax>0:
                e=min(e, cap/gmax)
            if e<=0: break
            z=y - e*g
            z=simplex_project(z)
            b_z=conv_square(z)
            m_z=masked_max(b_z,mask)
            if m_z < m - 1e-14:
                # also compare to best_m? no
                accepted=True
                x_prev=x
                x=z
                b=b_z; m=m_z
                if m < best_m - 1e-14:
                    best_m=m; best_x=x.copy(); stall=0
                else:
                    stall += 1
                # adjust eta
                if m_z < m - 5e-6*max(1.0,m): # relative? m ~1e-4
                    eta=min(1.0, eta*1.05)
                else:
                    eta=min(1.0, max(0.05, eta*1.0))
                break
        if not accepted:
            eta=max(0.02, eta*0.75)
            x_prev=x.copy()
            t=1.0
            stall += 1
            it_failed=...
        it +=1
        if stall > restart_every:
            # restart from best with noise
            noise_scale=0.01 + 0.01*(stall//restart_every)
            x_r=best_x + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, n)*best_x? Multiplying by best_x maybe. Use absolute 0.005/n? If sum1, noise std 0.002? Let's choose `pert = rng.normal(0, 0.02, n) * best_x` relative noise; then add small absolute 1e-6. Then clip/normalize.
            x_r = best_x + rng.normal(0, 0.02, n)*best_x + rng.normal(0, 1e-6, n)
            x_r = np.maximum(x_r,0); s=sum; normalize
            b_r=conv_square(x_r); m_r=masked_max
            if m_r < m: x=x_r;b=b_r;m=m_r
            else: x=best_x.copy();b=b_r? use best_b? We didn't store best_b. Compute best_b=best_x conv? We have not. Store best_b.
            x_prev=x.copy(); t=1.0; eta=eta_start; stall=0
        if verbose and it % 2000 ==0: print internal estimate 2*n*best_m? But mask may subset. Print `2*n*m`.
    return best_x,best_m
```
Need store best_b for restart. Set `best_b=b.copy()`. On update best_b=b_z.copy(). On return best_x,best_m.

```
def try_update(best_seq,best_score,a,mask,label):
    if a is None: return best_seq,best_score
    arr=norm_sum1(a)
    if arr.sum()<1e-12: return
    b=conv_square(arr); internal=masked_max(b,mask)
    est=2.0*len(arr)*internal
    score=safe_eval(arr)
    improved = np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score
    if improved:
       best_seq=arr.copy(); best_score=score
       print("NEW BEST "+label+": score="+repr(score)+" est="+repr(est)+" n="+str(len(arr)))
    else:
       print("tried "+label+": internal="+repr(internal)+" score="+repr(score))
    # sym
    sym=norm_sum1(0.5*(arr+arr[::-1]))
    if np.allclose(sym,arr,atol=1e-12): pass
    else:
       b2=conv_square(sym); int2=masked_max(b2,mask)
       if 2*len(sym)*int2 < est*0.999: # maybe
           score2=safe_eval(sym); if finite and <best: update
    return
```

Maybe we can use *alternating minimization in frequency*: Since b = ifft(A*A). We can set A phase to reduce b peaks. At each step, given current b peaks, adjust phase/amplitude? The gradient method is time domain. Spectral method may find global. Let's design a more effective spectral flat factor:

We want minimize ||b - c||∞ with c=1/(2n-1). This is like find nonnegative a with given polynomial square close to c. We can do iterative proportional fitting (IPF): In frequency, exact square target T. Start with A (FFT of a). Update A = sqrt(T / (A*A)?)? Since we need A^2≈T. If we fix phase, amplitude = sqrt(|T|). Iteration: A_new = sqrt(T) * exp(i angle(A))? This replaces amplitude with ideal, keeps phase. In time, clip nonnegative. This is like "spectral Floater". Could be powerful.
Algorithm:
```

  A=fft(a,L)
  # desired complex T = fft(b_target)
  # We want A^2 close T. Use amplitude ideal, phase current: A_new = sqrt(abs(T)+eps) * exp(1j angle(A))

Actually to move A^2 toward T, we can update `A_new = A * sqrt(T / A^2)` = sqrt(A^2 * T), i.e., geometric mean of current A^2 and T in frequency, phase averaged. In terms of A: `A_new = A * exp(0.5*(log(T)-2log(A)))` not stable. Simpler: `A_new = np.sqrt(T * A*A?)`? If A^2 current C. We want C_new = C * (T/C)^γ = C^{1-γ} T^γ. Then A_new = A * (T/C)^{γ/2}. Compute ratio = T / (A*A) (with epsilon), phase and magnitude. `A_new = A * ratio**(γ/2)`. This shifts spectrum toward target while preserving phase partly. This may be robust. In time, inverse clip.

Spectral iteration function:
```
def spectral_flat_iteration(a0, mask, n_iters=100, gamma=0.25, L=None):
    n=len; size=2n-1; L=pow2(size)
    a=norm_sum1(a0)
    b_target=np.zeros(L); b_target[:size]=1.0/size  # if mask full. If mask not, could target uniform over mask? no.
    T=np.fft.fft(b_target,L)
    A=np.fft.fft(np.pad(a,(0,L-n)),L)
    best=a; best_m=masked_max(conv_square(a),mask)
    for it in range(n_iters):
       C=A*A
       mag_C=np.abs(C); mag_T=np.abs(T)
       mag_C=np.maximum(mag_C,1e-30); mag_T=np.maximum(mag_T,1e-30)
       mag_new=np.exp((1-gamma)*np.log(mag_C)+gamma*np.log(mag_T))
       an_C=np.angle(C); an_T=np.angle(T)
       diff=an_T - an_C
       diff=(diff+np.pi) % (2*np.pi) - np.pi
       an_new=an_C + gamma*diff
       C_new=mag_new*np.exp(1j*an_new)
       # enforce Hermitian on C? C from real should be Hermitian; maybe average
       C_new=0.5*(C_new + np.conj(C_new[(-np.arange(L))%L]))
       A_new=np.sqrt(C_new)
       # enforce Hermitian A
       A_new=0.5*(A_new + np.conj(A_new[(-np.arange(L))%L]))
       a_new=np.fft.ifft(A_new).real[:n]
       a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0); s=a_new.sum(); if s<1e-12: break
       a_new/=s
       m=masked_max(conv_square(a_new),mask)
       if m<best_m: best=a_new; best_m=m
       # maybe accept only if m improves? For iteration, even if worse can escape? Use always update A? Could get stuck. Use if m < best_m (save best), but continue from new if finite. Maybe mix damping: a_next=0.5*a+0.5*a_new? To avoid negative.
       # We can blend time: a_blend=0.5*a+0.5*a_new normalize, use as next if finite.
       a = a_new # or blend
    return best,best_m
```

```
def spectral_flat_iteration(start, mask, n_iters=200, gamma=0.2, time_limit=60.0):
   t0=time.monotonic()
   a=norm_sum1(start); n=len; L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   bt=np.zeros(L); bt[:2*n-1]=1.0/(2*n-1)
   T=np.fft.fft(bt,L)
   m=masked_max(conv_square(a),mask)
   best=a.copy(); best_m=m
   A=np.fft.fft(np.pad(a,(0,L-n)),L)
   for it in range(n_iters):
      if time.time? break
      C=A*A
      # if C all zeros break
      mag_C=np.abs(C); mag_T=np.abs(T)
      eps=1e-30
      mag_C=np.maximum(mag_C,eps); mag_T=np.maximum(mag_T,eps)
      log_new=(1-gamma)*np.log(mag_C)+gamma*np.log(mag_T)
      # prevent overflow: log_new maybe min -700 max 700; clip
      log_new=np.clip(log_new,-700,700)
      mag_new=np.exp(log_new)
      an_C=np.angle(C); an_T=np.angle(T)
      diff=an_T-an_C
      diff=np.where(diff>np.pi,diff-2*np.pi,diff)
      diff=np.where(diff<-np.pi,diff+2*np.pi,diff)
      an_new=an_C+gamma*diff
      C_new=mag_new*np.exp(1j*an_new)
      # enforce hermitian
      mirror=np.conj(C_new[(-np.arange(L))%L])
      C_new=0.5*(C_new+mirror)
      A_new=np.sqrt(C_new)
      mirrorA=np.conj(A_new[(-np.arange(L))%L])
      A_new=0.5*(A_new+mirrorA)
      a_new=np.fft.ifft(A_new).real[:n]
      a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0)
      s=a_new.sum()
      if s<1e-12: break
      a_new/=s
      # blend with current to avoid discontinuity
      a_next=0.5*a + 0.5*a_new
      a_next=np.maximum(a_next,0); s2=a_next.sum(); if s2<... break; a_next/=s2
      m_new=masked_max(conv_square(a_next),mask)
      if m_new < best_m - 1e-15:
          best_m=m_new; best=a_next.copy()
      # Decide next: if m_new < m: a=a_next; else maybe a=0.8a+0.2a_next
      if m_new <= m + 1e-12:
          a=a_next
      else:
          a= a + 0.1*(a_next-a); normalize
      m=m_new
   return best,best_m
```

Now, `cyclic_shift_search`: For full mask, cost invariant under reversal? Not shift. We can try all if n<=2000, else 200 random. To avoid too many conv_square, time limit.
```
def cyclic_shift_search(a0, mask, max_trials=200, time_limit=30.0):
    a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len; m=masked_max(conv_square(a),mask); best=a.copy()
    if n <= 2000:
       idxs=range(1,n)
    else:
       rng=np.random.default_rng(99)
       idxs=rng.choice(np.arange(1,n), size=min(n-1,max_trials), replace=False)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    for s in idxs:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit: break
       a_s=np.roll(a,-int(s))
       m_s=masked_max(conv_square(a_s),mask)
       if m_s < m -1e-15: m=m_s; best=a_s.copy(); # keep updating base? If continue shift from best? We can continue from original to avoid chain? Use a_s from best maybe chain helps. For random, use original. For all, update m only, but eval next from original? If chain, roll best further. Use base=a0 for independent.
    return best
```
If shift improves, try further shifts around it? Not necessary.

```
def local_pair_search(a0,time_limit,mask):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    x=norm_sum1(a0); n=len
    b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max(b,mask)
    best=x.copy(); best_m=m
    rng=np.random.default_rng(555)
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        # active top 30 masked
        mask_idx=np.flatnonzero(mask)
        if len(mask_idx)==0: break
        b_sel=b[mask_idx]
        top_local=np.argpartition(b_sel, -30)[-30:]? Need top 30 largest.
        # simpler sort indices
        sel_idx=mask_idx[np.argsort(b_sel)[::-1][:30]]
        active=sel_idx.tolist()
        # risk
        risk=np.zeros(n)
        for k in active:
            ks=int(k)
            s=max(0,ks-n+1); e=min(n,ks+1)
            idx=np.arange(s,e)
            risk[s:e] += x[ks-idx]
        order=np.argsort(risk)
        sources=order[-40:]
        dests=order[:40]
        improved=False
        # precompute active arrays
        for trial in range(100):
            if time... break
            si=int(sources[rng.integers(len(sources))]); di=int(dests[rng.integers(len(dests))])
            if si==di or x[si]<1e-12: continue
            # precompute lists
            vals_s = [x[ks-si] if 0<=ks-si<n else 0.0 for ks in active]
            vals_d = [x[ks-di] if 0<=ks-di<n else 0.0 for ks in active]
            quads = [1.0 if si+di==ks else 0.0 for ks in active]
            best_delta=0; best_sur=m
            base_frac=[0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9]
            ai=x[si]
            for frac in base_frac:
                delta=frac*ai
                if delta<=0: continue
                sur=0.0
                # loop active use local arrays
                for j in range(len(active)):
                    c = b[active[j]] + 2.0*delta*(vals_d[j]-vals_s[j]) - 2.0*delta*delta*quads[j]
                    if c>sur: sur=c
                if sur < best_sur - 1e-15:
                    best_sur=sur; best_delta=delta
            if best_delta>0 and best_sur < m - 1e-13:
                x_try=x.copy(); x_try[si]-=best_delta; x_try[di]+=best_delta
                x_try=np.maximum(x_try,0); ssum=x_try.sum(); if ssum<1e-12: continue
                x_try/=ssum
                b_try=conv_square(x_try); m_try=masked_max(b_try,mask)
                if m_try < best_m - 1e-14:
                    x=x_try; b=b_try; m=m_try
                    best_m=m_try; best=x.copy(); improved=True; no_improve=0
                else:
                    no_improve+=1
        if not improved:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve>50: break
            # perturb
            pert=1e-3 + no_improve*1e-4
            x=best + rng.normal(0,pert,len(best))*best
            x=np.maximum(x,0); s=x.sum(); if s>0: x/=s
            b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max(b,mask)
            if m<best_m: best_m=m; best=x.copy()
    return best,best_m
```
Using Python list comprehension active 30 per trial okay. Could be slow if 100 trials*50 loops ~15k, okay.

Potential issue: `b[active[j]]` indexing list each time; convert `bs=[b[k] for k in active]`. Do that.

def lp_fw_search(a0,time_limit,mask,solver,initial_K=120,max_K=250,verbose=True):
    t0=time.monotonic(); x=norm_sum1(a0); n=len
    b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max(b,mask)
    best=x.copy(); best_m=m
    K=initial_K
    cap_K=max(20, min(max_K, 2000000//max(1,n)))
    K=min(K,cap_K)
    no_improve=0
    while time... and no_improve<200:
       mask_idx=np.flatnonzero(mask)
       if len(mask_idx)<K: K=max(20,len(mask_idx))
       b_sel=b[mask_idx]
       order=np.argsort(b_sel)[::-1][:K]
       active=mask_idx[order]
       A=np.zeros((len(active),n))
       for r,ks in enumerate(active):
           ks=int(ks); s=max(0,ks-n+1); e=min(n,ks+1)
           if s<e:
               A[r,s:e]=x[ks-np.arange(s,e)]
       zvar=cp.Variable(n, nonneg=True); Mvar=cp.Variable(nonneg=True)
       constraints=[cp.sum(zvar)==1, A @ zvar <= Mvar]
       prob=cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(Mvar),constraints)
       try: prob.solve(solver=solver, verbose=False)
       except Exception: break
       if prob.status not in ('optimal','optimal_inaccurate'): break
       z=np.asarray(zvar.value).ravel(); z=np.maximum(z,0); s=z.sum(); if s<1e-12: break; z/=s
       best_alpha=0; best_c=m
       for alpha in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.2,0.75):
           trial=(1-alpha)*x + alpha*z
           trial=np.maximum(trial,0)
           s2=trial.sum(); if s2<1e-12: continue
           trial/=s2
           b_t=conv_square(trial); c=masked_max(b_t,mask)
           if c < best_c - 1e-15:
               best_c=c; best_alpha=alpha; best_trial=trial.copy()
       if best_alpha>0 and best_c < m - 1e-14:
           x=best_trial; m=best_c; b=conv_square(x)
           if m < best_m -1e-14:
              best_m=m; best=x.copy(); no_improve=0
              K=min(cap_K,K+10)
           else: no_improve+=1
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           K=max(20,K//2)
       if verbose and iterations % 20==0: print
    return best,best_m
```

```
def safe_eval(a):
   try:
      arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
      arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
      arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
      s=arr.sum()
      if s<0.01: return float('inf')
      arr=arr/s
      out=[float(v) for v in arr]
      return float(evaluate_sequence(out))
   except Exception:
      return float('inf')
```

Let's define `build_mask(n, mtype)`:
```
def build_mask(n,mtype):
    nb=2*n-1
    mask=np.ones(nb,dtype=bool)
    if mtype=='no_edges':
       mask=np.zeros(nb,dtype=bool); mask[1:nb-1]=True
    elif mtype=='middle':
       mask=np.zeros(nb,dtype=bool); start=max(0,n//2); end=min(nb,3*n//2); mask[start:end]=True
    elif mtype=='center':
       mask=np.zeros(nb,dtype=bool); start=max(0,n-2); end=min(nb,n+2); mask[start:end]=True
    return mask
```

`choose_mask_type`:
```
def choose_mask_type(a,score):
  try:
    a=norm_sum1(a); n=len; b=conv_square(a); nb=2*n-1
    if n < 3: return 'full'
    Mest=score/(2.0*n) if np.isfinite(score) and score>0 else None
    cands=[('full', b.max()), ('no_edges', np.max(b[1:nb-1]) if nb>2 else b.max()), ('middle', np.max(b[n//2:3*n//2]) if ...)]
    if Mest is None: return 'full'
    best='full'; bestdiff=1e9
    for name,c in cands:
      if c>0:
        diff=abs(c-Mest)/max(abs(Mest),1e-30)
        if diff<bestdiff: bestdiff=diff; best=name
    if bestdiff < 0.05: return best
  except: pass
  return 'full'
```
If hidden full, Mest = b.max diff 0 => full. If hidden no_edges and endpoints small, no_edges maybe closer? If endpoints not max, full also close? If full cost differs from no_edges, choose. Good.

If Mest << b.max due hidden not scale, no candidate close, full default.

Now, let's think of possible numerical underflow in weights: `logw=(b_vals - m)/scale`; b_vals-m <=0, min maybe -large. Clip -50. But if mask false -inf, `logw=-inf`, exp0. ```
def central_binomial_candidate(n):
   a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
   for k in range(n-1):
       a[k+1]=a[k]*(2*k+1)/(2*k+2)
   return norm_sum1(a)
```

```
def try_update(best_seq,best_score,a,mask_idx,label):
    arr=norm_sum1(a)
    if arr.size==0: return
    b=conv_square(arr); m=masked_max_idx(b,mask_idx)
    est=2.0*arr.size*m
    score=safe_eval(arr)
    if np.isfinite(score) and score<best_score:
       best_seq=arr.copy(); best_score=score; print
    else: print
    # sym
    sym=norm_sum1(0.5*(arr+arr[::-1]))
    b2=conv_square(sym); m2=masked_max_idx(b2,mask_idx)
    if 2*arr.size*m2 < est*0.999:
       score2=safe_eval(sym)
       if finite and score2<best_score: update
    return
```
But if arr length changes, mask_idx length must correspond. Ensure.

def propose_candidate(budget_s=1800.0):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    total_budget=float(budget_s)
    time_limit=total_budget-60.0

    # shift search

       idx_new=make_mask_idx(new_n,mtype)
       a_l=interpolate_sequence(best_seq,new_n)
       # quick internal update? maybe update if est better? direct in try
       t5=min(rem*0.30,280)

    if finite and final_score<best_score: best...

Let's implement `deterministic_candidates(n, idx)`:
```
def deterministic_candidates(n):
   c=[]
   c.append(np.ones(n)/n)
   cb=central_binomial_candidate(n)
   c.append(cb)
   c.append(norm_sum1(0.5*(cb+cb[::-1])))
   # maybe smooth cb with window? 
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   c.append(norm_sum1(0.5+0.5*np.sin(np.pi*x)))
   c.append(norm_sum1(0.5+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*x)))
   # U-shape: 0.5+0.5*cos(2πx)? nonnegative min0 at 0.5
   c.append(norm_sum1(0.5+0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*x)))
   # exponential from center? 
   return c
```

```
"""
Optimizes nonnegative sequence minimizing max(np.convolve(a,a)) with sum normalization using reweighted projected gradient (Puma), LP-Frank-Wolfe, spectral flat-factor initialization, local pair search, and multi-length refinement.
"""
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    import cvxpy as cp
except Exception:
    cp = None
_SOLVER = None

`next_pow2`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```

`norm_sum1`:
```
def norm_sum1(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(arr)/max(1,arr.size)
    return arr/s
```

`simplex_project` as above. If arr empty? no.

`conv_square`:
```
def conv_square(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    size=2*n-1
    L=next_pow2(size)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:size]
    # Clean small negative
    c[c<0]=0.0
    return c
```
For n=1, size1,L1. rfft length1 works. c[0]=a0^2.

`make_mask_idx` handle n:
```
def make_mask_idx(n,mtype='full'):
    nb=2*int(n)-1
    if n<=0: return np.zeros(0,dtype=int)
    if mtype=='full' or mtype is None:
        return np.arange(nb,dtype=int)
    if mtype=='no_edges':
        if nb<=2: return np.arange(nb)
        return np.arange(1,nb-1,dtype=int)
    if mtype=='middle':
        start=max(0,int(n)//2)
        end=min(nb,3*int(n)//2)
        if end<=start: return np.arange(nb)
        return np.arange(start,end,dtype=int)
    if mtype=='center':
        start=max(0,int(n)-2)
        end=min(nb,int(n)+2)
        return np.arange(start,end,dtype=int)
    return np.arange(nb,dtype=int)
```

`masked_max_idx`:
```
def masked_max_idx(b,idx):
    if idx.size==0: return float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    return float(np.max(b[idx]))
```

`weights_b_idx`:
```
def weights_b_idx(b, idx, temp=0.1, bg=0.0):
    size=b.size
    p=np.zeros(size,dtype=np.float64)
    if idx.size==0:
        if size>0: p.fill(1.0/size)
        return p
    vals=b[idx]
    m=float(np.max(vals))
    if m <= 1e-300:
        p[idx]=1.0/idx.size
        return p
    scale=max(m*float(temp), 1e-300)
    logw=np.full(size, -1000.0, dtype=np.float64)
    diff=(vals-m)/scale
    diff=np.clip(diff, -100.0, 0.0)
    logw[idx]=diff
    w=np.exp(logw)
    w[~np.isclose(np.arange(size,dtype=bool),False?) can't.
```
Can't use `~` with idx easily? We can do `w = np.where(idx_bool, w, 0)`. But creating bool each time. Since logw for nonidx -1000 exp 0 underflows to 0 exactly? exp(-1000)=0? In numpy, exp(-1000) underflows to 0. So no need zero. But to be safe, `w[idx]=w[idx];` nonidx zero. Since logw full -1000, exp returns 0. okay.
```
    wsum=w.sum()
    if wsum <=0: p[idx]=1/idx.size; return p
    p=w/wsum
    if bg>0:
        p=(1-bg)*p
        p[idx]=p[idx] + (bg/idx.size)  # p has zeros outside
    # fix sum? adding uniform shifts sum: (1-bg)+bg=1, good.
    return p
```

`grad_weighted`:
```
def grad_weighted(a,p):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    size_p=p.size
    L=next_pow2(n + size_p -1)
    p_rev=np.ascontiguousarray(p[::-1])
    A=np.fft.rfft(np.ascontiguousarray(a), L)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p_rev, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*P, L)
    start=2*n-2
    stop=n-2
    if start >= stop:
       h=c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]? Need handle n=1.
```
For n=1, size_p=1, start=0, stop=-1? We need h length1 index m=2n-2-i =0. `c[0: -? : -1]` problematic. Use slice: `h = c[n-1:2*n-1]` length n? For n=3, c[2:5] indices 2,3,4 -> reversed needed. For general, h = c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]? For n=3, c[2:5]=[2,3,4] reversed [4,3,2]. Good. For n=1, c[0:1]=[0] reversed [0]. Great! So simpler: `h = c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]`. Check length: stop 2n-1 exclusive, from n-1 inclusive, length n. Good.
```
    h = c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    g=2.0*h
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    return g
```
For n=2, c indices from 1 to3? [1,2] reversed [2,1]. Need h0=conv index2? earlier m=2n-2=2, h1=m1. Yes. Good.

def safe_eval(a):
   try:
       arr=norm_sum1(a)
       if arr.size==0: return inf
       out=[float(x) for x in arr]
       val=evaluate_sequence(out)
       return float(val)
   except Exception:
       return float('inf')
```

`choose_mask_type` as above:
```
def choose_mask_type(a, score):
    try:
       arr=norm_sum1(a); n=arr.size
       if n<3: return 'full'
       b=conv_square(arr); nb=2*n-1
       if not np.isfinite(score) or score<=0: return 'full'
       M=score/(2.0*n)
       c_full=float(np.max(b))
       c_no=float(np.max(b[1:nb-1])) if nb>2 else c_full
       start=max(0,n//2); end=min(nb,3*n//2)
       c_mid=float(np.max(b[start:end])) if end>start else c_full
       best='full'; bestdiff=1e9
       for name,c in [('full',c_full),('no_edges',c_no),('middle',c_mid)]:
          if c>0:
             diff=abs(c-M)/max(M,1e-30)
             if diff<bestdiff: bestdiff=diff; best=name
       if bestdiff < 0.05: return best
       return 'full'
    except Exception:
       return 'full'
```
If hidden full, M=c_full -> full. If no_edges and endpoints not max, c_full and c_no same, full chosen (tie first). Fine. If endpoints are max but hidden excludes, M=c_no<c_full, no_edges chosen. Good.

`central_binomial_candidate(n)`:
```
def central_binomial_candidate(n):
    a=np.empty(max(1,int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    a[0]=1.0
    for k in range(1,a.size):
       a[k]=a[k-1]*((2.0*k-1.0)/(2.0*k))
    return norm_sum1(a)
```
If n=0? not.

`deterministic_candidates(n)`:
```
def deterministic_candidates(n):
    c=[]
    n=int(n)
    if n<=0: return []
    c.append(np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)/n)
    cb=central_binomial_candidate(n)
    c.append(cb)
    c.append(norm_sum1(0.5*(cb+cb[::-1])))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    c.append(norm_sum1(0.5+0.5*np.sin(np.pi*x)))
    c.append(norm_sum1(0.5+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*x)))
    c.append(norm_sum1(0.5+0.5*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x)))
    # maybe exponential decay both ends
    center=np.abs(x-0.5)
    c.append(norm_sum1(1.0 - 0.8*np.sin(np.pi*x)**2))
    return c
```
Remove duplicates? not necessary; internal sort.

`try_update`:
```
def try_update(best_seq,best_score,a,idx,label):
    if a is None: return best_seq,best_score
    arr=norm_sum1(a)
    if arr.size==0: return
    b=conv_square(arr)
    m=masked_max_idx(b,idx)
    est=2.0*arr.size*m
    score=safe_eval(arr)
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score + 1e-15:
        best_seq=arr.copy(); best_score=score
        print("NEW BEST "+label+": score="+repr(score)+" est="+repr(est)+" n="+str(arr.size))
    else:
        print("tried "+label+": est="+repr(est)+" score="+repr(score)+" best="+repr(best_score))
    # sym
    sym=norm_sum1(0.5*(arr+arr[::-1]))
    if not np.allclose(sym, arr, rtol=1e-14, atol=1e-15):
       b2=conv_square(sym); m2=masked_max_idx(b2,idx)
       est2=2.0*sym.size*m2
       if est2 < est - 1e-10*max(1.0,est):
           score2=safe_eval(sym)
           if np.isfinite(score2) and score2 < best_score + 1e-15:
               best_seq=sym.copy(); best_score=score2
               print("NEW BEST sym "+label+": score="+repr(score2)+" n="+str(sym.size))
    return best_seq,best_score
```
If `idx` length not arr.size? Should match. If not, `masked_max` may error. Ensure.

`cyclic_shift_search`:
```
def cyclic_shift_search(a0, idx, max_trials=200, time_limit=30.0):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    a=norm_sum1(a0); n=a.size
    b=conv_square(a); m=masked_max_idx(b,idx)
    best=a.copy()
    if n<=1: return best,m
    if n <= 2000:
       shifts=list(range(1,n))
    else:
       rng=np.random.default_rng(12345)
       if n-1 <= max_trials: shifts=list(range(1,n))
       else: shifts=[int(s) for s in rng.choice(np.arange(1,n), size=max_trials, replace=False)]
    for s in shifts:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit: break
       a_s=np.roll(a,-s)
       m_s=masked_max_idx(conv_square(a_s),idx)
       if m_s < m - 1e-15:
          m=m_s; best=a_s.copy()
    return best,m
```
Note if a_s improves, subsequent shifts from original? We use original `a`. okay. Could also continue from best chain: not.

`spectral_flat_iteration` as above. Need if idx.size==0? return.
```
def spectral_flat_iteration(start, idx, n_iters=200, gamma=0.2, time_limit=60.0):
   t0=time.monotonic()
   a=norm_sum1(start); n=a.size
   if n==0: return a,0
   size=2*n-1; L=next_pow2(size)
   bt=np.zeros(L,dtype=np.float64)
   if idx.size>0:
      bt[idx]=1.0/idx.size
   else:
      bt[:size]=1.0/size
   T=np.fft.fft(bt,L)
   m=masked_max_idx(conv_square(a),idx)
   best=a.copy(); best_m=m
   A=np.fft.fft(np.pad(a,(0,L-n)),L)
   all_idx=np.arange(L)
   mirror_idx=(-all_idx)%L
   for it in range(int(n_iters)):
      if L? time break
      C=A*A
      mag_C=np.abs(C); mag_T=np.abs(T)
      eps=1e-300
      mag_C=np.maximum(mag_C,eps); mag_T=np.maximum(mag_T,eps)
      log_new=(1-gamma)*np.log(mag_C)+gamma*np.log(mag_T)
      log_new=np.clip(log_new,-700,700)
      mag_new=np.exp(log_new)
      an_C=np.angle(C); an_T=np.angle(T)
      diff=an_T-an_C
      diff=np.where(diff>np.pi, diff-2*np.pi, diff)
      diff=np.where(diff<-np.pi, diff+2*np.pi, diff)
      an_new=an_C + gamma*diff
      C_new=mag_new*np.exp(1j*an_new)
      C_new=0.5*(C_new + np.conj(C_new[mirror_idx]))
      A_new=np.sqrt(C_new)
      A_new=0.5*(A_new + np.conj(A_new[mirror_idx]))
      a_new=np.fft.ifft(A_new).real[:n]
      a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
      s=a_new.sum()
      if s<1e-15: break
      a_new=a_new/s
      a_next=0.5*a + 0.5*a_new
      a_next=np.maximum(a_next,0.0)
      s2=a_next.sum()
      if s2<1e-15: break
      a_next=a_next/s2
      m_new=masked_max_idx(conv_square(a_next),idx)
      if m_new < best_m - 1e-15:
         best_m=m_new; best=a_next.copy()
      if m_new <= m + 1e-12:
         a=a_next
      else:
         a=a + 0.1*(a_next-a)
         a=np.maximum(a,0); s3=a.sum(); if s3>0: a=a/s3
      m=m_new
   return best,best_m
```
Potential: `bt[idx]` if idx int array okay. If idx length large. Good.

`puma_search` as above. Need use `idx` not bool.

Detailed puma code:
```
def puma_search(start, time_limit, idx, temp_start=0.3, temp_end=0.02, eta_start=0.4, bg_start=0.03, restart_every=5000, verbose=True):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    if time_limit <=0: return norm_sum1(start),0
    x=norm_sum1(start)
    n=x.size
    x_prev=x.copy()
    b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max_idx(b,idx)
    best_m=m; best_x=x.copy(); best_b=b.copy()
    eta=float(eta_start); t=1.0; it=0; stall=0; fails=0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(777)
    step_cap=0.08
    last_print=t0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        frac=(time.monotonic()-t0)/max(time_limit,1e-9)
        if frac>1: frac=1
        temp=temp_start + (temp_end-temp_start)*frac
        bg=bg_start*(1.0-0.8*frac)
        if it>1:
            t_new=(1.0+np.sqrt(1.0+4.0*t*t))/2.0
            theta=(t-1.0)/t_new
            if theta>0.9: theta=0.9
            if fails>0: theta=0.5*theta
            y=x + theta*(x - x_prev)
            t=float(t_new)
        else:
            y=x.copy(); t=1.0
        y=simplex_project(y)
        us=y.sum()
        if us<=0: y=x.copy()
        else: y=y/us
        p=weights_b_idx(b, idx, temp, bg)
        g=grad_weighted(y,p)
        gmax=float(np.max(g))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax < 1e-300:
            break
        accepted=False
        step_factors=(1.0,0.5,0.25)
        for factor in step_factors:
            e=eta*factor
            if gmax>0:
                e=min(e, step_cap/gmax)
            if e<=1e-15:
                break
            z=y - e*g
            z=simplex_project(z)
            zs=z.sum()
            if zs<=0: continue
            z=z/zs
            b_z=conv_square(z)
            m_z=masked_max_idx(b_z,idx)
            if m_z < m - 1e-14:
                accepted=True
                x_prev=x
                x=z
                b=b_z
                m=m_z
                if m < best_m - 1e-14:
                    best_m=m; best_x=x.copy(); best_b=b.copy(); stall=0
                else:
                    stall += 1
                # adaptive eta
                if m - m_z > 1e-7*max(1e-300, best_m):
                    eta=min(1.2, eta*1.03)
                else:
                    eta=max(0.05, eta*0.98)
                fails=0
                break
        if not accepted:
            eta=max(0.02, eta*0.75)
            x_prev=x.copy()
            t=1.0
            fails += 1
            stall += 1
        it += 1
        if stall >= restart_every:
            noise=0.02 + 0.01*(stall//restart_every)
            pert=rng.normal(0.0, noise, n) * best_x
            x_r=best_x + pert + rng.normal(0.0, 1e-6, n)
            x_r=np.maximum(x_r,0.0)
            sr=x_r.sum()
            if sr>1e-15:
                x_r=x_r/sr
                b_r=conv_square(x_r)
                m_r=masked_max_idx(b_r,idx)
                if m_r < m:
                    x=x_r; b=b_r; m=m_r
                else:
                    x=best_x.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); m=best_m
            else:
                x=best_x.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); m=best_m
            x_prev=x.copy(); t=1.0; eta=float(eta_start); stall=0; fails=0
        if verbose and time.monotonic()-last_print > 20.0:
            last_print=time.monotonic()
            print("Puma it="+str(it)+" t="+repr(round(time.monotonic()-t0,1))+"s est="+repr(2.0*n*best_m)+" m="+repr(best_m))
    # final sym? maybe return best_x and best_m
    return best_x,best_m
```
Potential bug: `m - m_z` on accepted: m is old x cost; m_z new. Good. But after update `m=m_z`, the condition uses old m? In code above, `if m - m_z >` before `m=m_z`? I placed after update? Need order. Use old_m variable before update. Better:
```
old_m=m
... if m_z < old_m - tol:
   ... if m - m_z > ... # m still old
```
We'll implement with old_m.

Also `stall` should count no global best. If accepted and best improves, stall=0; if accepted but not best, stall++. On fail stall++. Good.

`step_cap=0.08`; if gmax very small, e not capped. Good.

Momentum: On fail, x_prev=x; t=1; fails++. On accept, x_prev=old x. Good.

`bg` can become negative if frac>1.25? frac max1, bg positive. good.

`local_pair_search` with idx:
Need active top from idx. Use `np.argsort(b[idx])[::-1][:30]`. If idx large, okay. For b[idx] view.
```
def local_pair_search(a0,time_limit,idx):
  t0=time.monotonic(); x=norm_sum1(a0); n=x.size
  b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max_idx(b,idx)
  best=x.copy(); best_m=m
  rng=np.random.default_rng(4242)
  no_improve=0
  if idx.size==0: return best,best_m
  frac_list=(0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9)
  while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
     sel=b[idx]
     order=np.argsort(sel)[::-1]
     top=min(30, order.size)
     active=idx[order[:top]].astype(int).tolist()
     active_b=[float(b[k]) for k in active]
     risk=np.zeros(n)
     for k in active:
       ks=int(k); s=max(0,ks-n+1); e=min(n,ks+1)
       if s<e:
          idxs=np.arange(s,e)
          risk[s:e] += x[ks-idxs]
     ord_risk=np.argsort(risk)
     sources=ord_risk[-40:]
     dests=ord_risk[:40]
     if sources.size==0 or dests.size==0: break
     improved=False
     for trial in range(80):
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit: break
       si=int(sources[rng.integers(sources.size)])
       di=int(dests[rng.integers(dests.size)])
       if si==di or x[si]<=1e-14: continue
       # precompute arrays
       vals_s=np.empty(len(active)); vals_d=np.empty(len(active)); quads=np.empty(len(active))
       for j,k in enumerate(active):
          ks=int(k)
          j_s=ks-si; j_d=ks-di
          vals_s[j]= x[j_s] if 0<=j_s<n else 0.0
          vals_d[j]= x[j_d] if 0<=j_d<n else 0.0
          quads[j]= 1.0 if si+di==ks else 0.0
       ai=float(x[si])
       best_delta=0.0; best_sur=m
       for frac in frac_list:
          delta=frac*ai
          if delta<=0: continue
          # max over active vectorized
          c=active_b + 2.0*delta*(vals_d - vals_s) - 2.0*delta*delta*quads
          sur=float(np.max(c))
          if sur < best_sur - 1e-15:
             best_sur=sur; best_delta=delta
       if best_delta>0 and best_sur < m - 1e-13:
          x_try=x.copy(); x_try[si]-=best_delta; x_try[di]+=best_delta
          x_try=np.maximum(x_try,0.0); ss=x_try.sum()
          if ss<=0: continue
          x_try/=ss
          b_try=conv_square(x_try); m_try=masked_max_idx(b_try,idx)
          if m_try < best_m - 1e-14:
             x=x_try; b=b_try; m=m_try
             best_m=m_try; best=x.copy(); improved=True; no_improve=0
          else:
             no_improve+=1
     if not improved:
        no_improve +=1
        if no_improve > 40: break
        noise=1e-3 + 1e-4*no_improve
        x=best + rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)*best
        x=np.maximum(x,0.0); ss=x.sum()
        if ss>0:
           x=x/ss
           b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max_idx(b,idx)
           if m<best_m: best_m=m; best=x.copy()
  return best,best_m
```
Vectorized c uses lists converted to np arrays? active_b list, vals arrays, okay. `np.max(c)` each fraction. Good.

`lp_fw_search` with cp global. Need if cp is None. Use `int(n)`.
```
def lp_fw_search(a0,time_limit,idx,solver,initial_K=120,max_K=250,verbose=True):
   t0=time.monotonic()
   x=norm_sum1(a0); n=x.size
   b=conv_square(x); m=masked_max_idx(b,idx)
   best=x.copy(); best_m=m
   if idx.size==0 or solver is None or cp is None: return best,best_m
   cap_K=max(30, min(int(max_K), 2000000//max(1,n)))
   K=min(int(initial_K), cap_K)
   no_improve=0; it=0; last_print=t0
   while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit and no_improve < 200:
      if idx.size < K: K=idx.size
      if K <=0: break
      sel=b[idx]
      order=np.argsort(sel)[::-1][:K]
      active=idx[order]
      if active.size ==0: break
      A=np.zeros((active.size,n), dtype=np.float64)
      for r in range(active.size):
         ks=int(active[r])
         s=max(0,ks-n+1); e=min(n,ks+1)
         if s<e:
            A[r,s:e]=x[ks-np.arange(s,e)]
      zvar=cp.Variable(n, nonneg=True)
      Mvar=cp.Variable(nonneg=True)
      constraints=[cp.sum(zvar)==1.0, A @ zvar <= Mvar]
      prob=cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(Mvar), constraints)
      try:
         prob.solve(solver=solver, verbose=False)
      except Exception:
         break
      if prob.status not in ('optimal','optimal_inaccurate'):
         break
      try:
         z=np.asarray(zvar.value, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
      except Exception:
         break
      z=np.maximum(z,0.0); sz=z.sum()
      if sz<1e-12: break
      z=z/sz
      best_alpha=0.0; best_c=m; best_trial=None
      for alpha in (1.0,0.75,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.2):
         trial=(1.0-alpha)*x + alpha*z
         trial=np.maximum(trial,0.0)
         st=trial.sum()
         if st<1e-12: continue
         trial=trial/st
         b_t=conv_square(trial); c_t=masked_max_idx(b_t,idx)
         if c_t < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c=c_t; best_alpha=alpha; best_trial=trial
      if best_trial is not None and best_c < m - 1e-14:
         x=best_trial; m=best_c; b=conv_square(x)
         if m < best_m - 1e-14:
            best_m=m; best=x.copy(); no_improve=0
            K=min(cap_K, K+10)
         else:
            no_improve +=1
      else:
         no_improve +=1
         K=max(30, K//2)
      it +=1
      if verbose and time.monotonic()-last_print > 30.0:
         last_print=time.monotonic()
         print("LP-FW it="+str(it)+" est="+repr(2.0*n*best_m)+" K="+str(K))
   return best,best_m
```
Dense A @ zvar in cvxpy with A ndarray. If n large and active 100, okay. If solve too slow, time cap. It doesn't check time inside solve; if one solve > time_limit, still okay as long total. Could be long. Limit K by 2M entries for solve. Good.

def get_solver():
    global _SOLVER
    if _SOLVER is not None: return _SOLVER
    if cp is None: return None

        if solver is None: continue
        try:
            x=cp.Variable(10, nonneg=True)
            prob=cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cp.sum(x)), [cp.sum(x)==1, x<=0.5])
            prob.solve(solver=solver, verbose=False)
            if prob.status in ('optimal','optimal_inaccurate'):
                _SOLVER=solver
                return solver
        except Exception:
            continue
    return None
